q-0001simple3I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?You need to update MOM **within 2 weeks** of changing your residential address [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]. Since you moved last week, you still have time, but should do this promptly to avoid penalties. **How to update:** 1. Ensure your new address meets the relevant [housing requirements](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/work-permit-for-foreign-worker/housing/various-types-of-housing) 2. Log in to [EP eService](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-pass-eservice) using your Singpass to update your address—the update is immediate 3. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]The answer covers all key points: 2-week deadline, penalty warning, housing requirements check, EP eService login, immediate update, and printing the acknowledgement page. Substantively equivalent to the reference answer.
Question:
I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?
Expected:
You need to update MOM within 2 weeks of changing your residential address. Otherwise, you may be penalised. To update: (1) Ensure that your new address meets the relevant housing requirements. (2) Log in to EP eService to update your address. The update is immediate. (3) Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card.
Actual:
You need to update MOM **within 2 weeks** of changing your residential address [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]. Since you moved last week, you still have time, but should do this promptly to avoid penalties.

**How to update:**

1. Ensure your new address meets the relevant [housing requirements](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/work-permit-for-foreign-worker/housing/various-types-of-housing)
2. Log in to [EP eService](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-pass-eservice) using your Singpass to update your address—the update is immediate
3. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]

q-0002simple3We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?For EP (Sponsorship) renewal, here's the timeline you need to know: **When you can start renewing:** You can begin applying to renew **3 months before the pass expires** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. **Processing time:** It takes around **8 weeks to process the renewal application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]. For most cases, you can expect a decision **within 6 weeks** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. **IPA letter validity:** Once your renewal is successful, you'll receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for **3 months** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or current pass expires, whichever comes first, to avoid an overstaying fine.The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, 8-week/6-week processing times, 3-month IPA validity, and the requirement to issue the pass before IPA or current pass expiry to avoid overstaying fines. While it omits minor details about remaining validity carry-over and max 3-year renewal duration, these were not part of the user's question and the essential information is fully addressed.
Question:
We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?
Expected:
For EP (Sponsorship), you can start applying to renew 3 months before the pass expires (as opposed to the standard EP, which allows renewal up to 6 months / 180 days before expiry). You must apply before the expiry date. Processing time for EP (Sponsorship) renewals is within 6 weeks for most cases (the renewal form itself states around 8 weeks). Once the renewal is approved, you will receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for 3 months. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or the current pass expires, whichever occurs first — otherwise an overstaying fine will be imposed. The duration of the renewed pass will start when the current pass expires. Any remaining validity from the current pass (up to six months) is added on to the renewal, so renewing early does not reduce or lose validity. The renewed pass can be granted for up to 3 years.
Actual:
For EP (Sponsorship) renewal, here's the timeline you need to know:

**When you can start renewing:**
You can begin applying to renew **3 months before the pass expires** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md].

**Processing time:**
It takes around **8 weeks to process the renewal application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]. For most cases, you can expect a decision **within 6 weeks** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md].

**IPA letter validity:**
Once your renewal is successful, you'll receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for **3 months** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or current pass expires, whichever comes first, to avoid an overstaying fine.
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, 8-week/6-week processing times, 3-month IPA validity, and the requirement to issue the pass before IPA or current pass expiry to avoid overstaying fines. While it omits minor details about remaining validity carry-over and max 3-year renewal duration, these were not part of the user's question and the essential information is fully addressed.

q-0003simple3I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify, and does it change depending on the candidate's age or industry?# Employment Pass Minimum Salary Requirements Yes, the minimum qualifying salary for an Employment Pass (EP) varies by both age and industry. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] ## Current Requirements (Before 1 Jan 2027) **General sectors (except financial services):** Starting at $5,600 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing progressively with age to $10,700 for those aged 45 and above. **Financial services sector:** Starting at $6,200 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing progressively with age to $11,800 for those aged 45 and above. ## New Requirements (From 1 Jan 2027 onwards) **General sectors:** Starting at $6,000 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing to $11,500 for those aged 45 and above. **Financial services:** Starting at $6,600 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing to $12,700 for those aged 45 and above. These salary requirements apply to both new applications and pass renewals. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] Note that meeting the minimum qualifying salary is only Stage 1 of the eligibility framework—candidates must also pass the points-based COMPASS assessment (Stage 2) unless exempted.The chatbot's answer covers all key points from the reference: the $5,600 starting salary, progressive increase with age up to $10,700 for mid-40s, and higher thresholds for financial services. It also adds useful detail about upcoming 2027 changes and the COMPASS framework, which is accurate and helpful rather than misleading.
Question:
I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify, and does it change depending on the candidate's age or industry?
Expected:
The qualifying salary for an Employment Pass is a fixed monthly salary comparable to the top one-third of local PMET salaries. It starts from $5,600 and increases progressively with age, up to $10,700 for candidates in their mid-40s. Candidates in the financial services sector need higher salaries than these amounts to qualify.
Actual:
# Employment Pass Minimum Salary Requirements

Yes, the minimum qualifying salary for an Employment Pass (EP) varies by both age and industry. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

## Current Requirements (Before 1 Jan 2027)

**General sectors (except financial services):** Starting at $5,600 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing progressively with age to $10,700 for those aged 45 and above.

**Financial services sector:** Starting at $6,200 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing progressively with age to $11,800 for those aged 45 and above.

## New Requirements (From 1 Jan 2027 onwards)

**General sectors:** Starting at $6,000 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing to $11,500 for those aged 45 and above.

**Financial services:** Starting at $6,600 for candidates aged 23 and below, increasing to $12,700 for those aged 45 and above.

These salary requirements apply to both new applications and pass renewals. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] Note that meeting the minimum qualifying salary is only Stage 1 of the eligibility framework—candidates must also pass the points-based COMPASS assessment (Stage 2) unless exempted.
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer covers all key points from the reference: the $5,600 starting salary, progressive increase with age up to $10,700 for mid-40s, and higher thresholds for financial services. It also adds useful detail about upcoming 2027 changes and the COMPASS framework, which is accurate and helpful rather than misleading.

q-0004simple3We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?Based on the knowledge base, here's what applies to your candidate's common-law partner: **Pass type:** The common-law spouse can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. **Working in Singapore:** Yes, common-law spouses holding an LTVP can work in Singapore by applying for a [Letter of Consent (LOC)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md). The application is free and typically takes up to 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. **Timing note:** The knowledge base recommends applying for family passes once the ONE Pass is approved, since LTVP applications take 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].The answer correctly identifies LTVP for the common-law spouse and LOC for working in Singapore, matching the reference answer, with helpful additional details about processing time and timing.
Question:
We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?
Expected:
A common-law spouse (defined as a common-law relationship between 2 people that is recognised under the laws of their country or region) of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder is eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP). Common-law spouses on LTVP can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what applies to your candidate's common-law partner:

**Pass type:** The common-law spouse can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].

**Working in Singapore:** Yes, common-law spouses holding an LTVP can work in Singapore by applying for a [Letter of Consent (LOC)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md). The application is free and typically takes up to 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md].

**Timing note:** The knowledge base recommends applying for family passes once the ONE Pass is approved, since LTVP applications take 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The answer correctly identifies LTVP for the common-law spouse and LOC for working in Singapore, matching the reference answer, with helpful additional details about processing time and timing.

q-0005simple2I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to know about Employment Pass applications: **Processing Time:** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md] - If you apply online: processed or given an update within **10 business days** - If your company is not registered in Singapore: within **6 weeks** for most cases **Application Fee:** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md] The fee is **$105**, which you can pay by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex when you submit the application. Note that you can check your application status online after 10 business days (or 6 weeks for overseas companies). If additional documents are required, your processing time may extend beyond these timeframes.Covers the key points (10 business days for online, 6 weeks for overseas, $105 fee, and note about extended processing). However, it lists GIRO as a payment method for both scenarios when the reference specifies GIRO for Singapore-registered companies and PayNow for overseas companies, and it doesn't mention the local sponsor requirement for overseas companies.
Question:
I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?
Expected:
The processing time and fee depend on whether the employer has a Singapore-registered company or not. For employers with a Singapore-registered company applying online via the EP eService, the application is processed or given an update within 10 business days, and the application fee is $105, payable by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company (sponsorship cases), a local sponsor must submit the application, the processing time is within 6 weeks for most cases (some cases may take longer), and the application fee is also $105, payable by PayNow, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. In either case, if additional information or documents are required, the processing time may take longer than the stated timeframes.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to know about Employment Pass applications:

**Processing Time:** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]
- If you apply online: processed or given an update within **10 business days**
- If your company is not registered in Singapore: within **6 weeks** for most cases

**Application Fee:** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]
The fee is **$105**, which you can pay by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex when you submit the application.

Note that you can check your application status online after 10 business days (or 6 weeks for overseas companies). If additional documents are required, your processing time may extend beyond these timeframes.
Judge remark (scored 2):
Covers the key points (10 business days for online, 6 weeks for overseas, $105 fee, and note about extended processing). However, it lists GIRO as a payment method for both scenarios when the reference specifies GIRO for Singapore-registered companies and PayNow for overseas companies, and it doesn't mention the local sponsor requirement for overseas companies.

q-0006simple3We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?Based on the knowledge base, non-listed companies cannot use company valuation to meet the "established company" criteria for the ONE Pass (Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass). [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md] For a company to be considered established, it must have **at least one of the following**: - Market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or - Annual revenue of at least US$200 million [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md] However, there is an alternative path: if your company does not meet these criteria, your candidate can **re-apply for the ONE Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months** with your company. Question:
We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?
Expected:
No, company valuation is not accepted as an alternative to market capitalisation. Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least US$200 million to be considered established companies, as they do not have market capitalisation. If the prospective employer does not meet the established company criteria, the candidate may still apply for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application. Alternatively, the candidate may apply for another work pass such as the Employment Pass, Personalised Employment Pass, EntrePass, or Tech.Pass.

q-0007simple3We're applying for an Employment Pass for a candidate whose qualification verification through a background screening company has been pending for over two weeks. The candidate's university is one of the institutions listed in the application form's drop-down list. Do we have to wait for the verification to come back before we can submit the EP application, or is there some workaround?You don't have to wait for the verification to come back. Since your candidate's institution is listed in the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit a **receipt for the verification service in lieu of the verification proof** if verification is still pending after 14 working days [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 2]. Here's what happens next: once your EP application is approved, you can bring in the candidate and get their pass issued without needing to submit the full verification proof. However, there's an important caveat—if the qualification is later found to be false, the work pass will be revoked and the candidate must leave Singapore [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 2].The chatbot's answer is substantively equivalent to the reference answer, covering all key points: the 14-working-day threshold, submitting the receipt in lieu of verification proof, no need to submit verification proof after approval, and the caveat about revocation if the qualification is false.
Question:
We're applying for an Employment Pass for a candidate whose qualification verification through a background screening company has been pending for over two weeks. The candidate's university is one of the institutions listed in the application form's drop-down list. Do we have to wait for the verification to come back before we can submit the EP application, or is there some workaround?
Expected:
If your verification is still pending after 14 working days and your awarding institution is from the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit the receipt for the verification service in lieu of the verification proof in the work pass application. Once the work pass application is approved, you can bring in the candidate and get their pass issued. You do not need to submit the verification proof to MOM after that. However, if the qualification is found to be false, the work pass will be revoked and the candidate must leave Singapore.
Actual:
You don't have to wait for the verification to come back. Since your candidate's institution is listed in the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit a **receipt for the verification service in lieu of the verification proof** if verification is still pending after 14 working days [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 2].

Here's what happens next: once your EP application is approved, you can bring in the candidate and get their pass issued without needing to submit the full verification proof. However, there's an important caveat—if the qualification is later found to be false, the work pass will be revoked and the candidate must leave Singapore [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 2].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer is substantively equivalent to the reference answer, covering all key points: the 14-working-day threshold, submitting the receipt in lieu of verification proof, no need to submit verification proof after approval, and the caveat about revocation if the qualification is false.

Question:
We have a candidate who earned S$30,000/month overseas for the past 12 months at a company with annual revenue of US$180 million and market cap of US$450 million. The company's global offices combined have annual revenue exceeding US$200 million though. Does the company qualify as established, and what documents do we need to submit for this candidate?
Expected:
For a company to be considered established, it must have at least one of the following: market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or annual revenue of at least US$200 million. Combined amounts from the entire global office can also be considered and will be assessed on a case-by-case basis. So while the individual office does not meet either threshold on its own, the combined global revenue exceeding US$200 million may qualify, but this will be assessed case-by-case by MOM.

For a candidate whose current or last held employment was overseas, the following documents are required:
1. Passport page (including any amendments). If the candidate's name in their passport differs from their name in other documents, an explanation letter and supporting documents such as a deed poll are also needed.
2. Monthly payslips for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application.
3. Employment verification proof from a background screening company.
4. Documents supporting other income sources other than fixed monthly salary (if applicable).
5. Latest financial information of the candidate's employer obtained within the last 3 months, including documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. If this information is not available, the company's latest audited Profit & Loss statements may be provided instead. MOM will only consider documents from third party auditors; documents signed by the company's management staff will not be accepted.

Additionally, if the candidate drew salaries from more than one employer in the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application, the qualifying fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 (or its equivalent in foreign currency) should come from one employer only. Other sources of income may be considered on a case-by-case basis.
Actual:
Based on the information provided, **the company does not qualify as established** under the standard criteria. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

For a company to be considered established, it must have at least one of the following:
- Market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or
- Annual revenue of at least US$200 million

Your candidate's employer has a market cap of US$450 million (below the US$500 million threshold) and annual revenue of US$180 million (below the US$200 million threshold). While the combined global offices exceed US$200 million in annual revenue, the knowledge base states this "will be assessed on a case-by-case basis," but does not confirm it automatically qualifies. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

**For documents**, since the candidate earned S$30,000/month overseas for 12 consecutive months, you would need to submit: [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/documents-required.md]

- Monthly payslips for the 12 consecutive months leading up to application
- Employment verification proof from a background screening company
- Latest financial information of the employer (within last 3 months), including market capitalisation or annual revenue documents
- Documents supporting any other income sources (if applicable)
- Passport page

I recommend contacting MOM directly to clarify whether the combined global revenue of US$200+ million would qualify this company as established.

q-0009simple3We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?To appoint an Employment Pass holder from another company to your Board of Directors, here's what you need to know [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md]: **Processing time:** Within 5 weeks. **Documents required:** The Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship. **Validity:** The approval is valid up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP. Note that you'll need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, and the approval is generally granted only if your company is related by shareholding to the EP holder's employer (as reflected in ACRA records) and the secondary directorship is related to their primary employment role.All key points are accurately covered: 5 weeks processing time, appointment letter as required document, validity tied to EP expiry/cancellation. Additional context about LOC and eligibility conditions is helpful and not misleading.
Question:
We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?
Expected:
The process takes within 5 weeks. The document required is the Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship. The validity of the Letter of Consent (LOC) is up to the expiry or cancellation date of the EP holder's current Employment Pass. When the EP is successfully renewed, you need to apply for a new LOC.
Actual:
To appoint an Employment Pass holder from another company to your Board of Directors, here's what you need to know [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md]:

**Processing time:** Within 5 weeks.

**Documents required:** The Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship.

**Validity:** The approval is valid up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP.

Note that you'll need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, and the approval is generally granted only if your company is related by shareholding to the EP holder's employer (as reflected in ACRA records) and the secondary directorship is related to their primary employment role.

Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus. What are the eligibility requirements for the candidate under the SOL for that role?
Expected:
For the carbon credit trader/broker role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both criteria (1) and (2): (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields. (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that the job duties for this role include trading or brokering carbon credits, originating and structuring carbon credit deals, facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits, and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors that affect price dynamics in over-the-counter markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). Additionally, the candidate needs to perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation, meet the additional requirements as stated in the SOL Employer Guide, and you need to select the shortage occupation in the EP application. The candidate should select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application form, which must be the same as the occupation for the job advertisement in MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.
Actual:
To claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus for a carbon credit trader role, your candidate must meet the following eligibility requirements [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]:

**Key Job Duties:**
Your candidate must perform the core duties of a carbon credit trader, which include:
- Trading or brokering carbon credits
- Originating and structuring carbon credit deals
- Facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits
- Performing carbon market research and analysis on carbon credit types, quality, price dynamics, and international regulations [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md § Carbon standards and methodology analyst]

**Additional Requirements:**
Your candidate must minimally meet **both** of the following criteria [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md § Carbon standards and methodology analyst]:

1. Minimum of three years' total work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields
2. At least a degree-equivalent qualification (scoring 10 points on COMPASS C2 Qualifications criteria)

q-0012simple2We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore and we want to know if we can qualify for the SEP bonus under COMPASS. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a VC that isn't EDBI or SEEDS Capital. Does that count, or does the funding have to come from specific investors?Based on the COMPASS criteria, your startup would **not** qualify for the SEP bonus under the current eligible programmes. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/annex-c---eligible-programmes-for-sep-bonus.md] The high-growth startup category requires two conditions to be met: - Incorporation in Singapore ✓ (you meet this) - Minimum investment of USD $10 million over the past 36 months ✓ (you meet this) - **Funding from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms** ✗ (your VC funding does not qualify) The funding source is a specific requirement—it must come from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms. A standard VC that isn't on this list does not satisfy the criterion, even if the investment amount exceeds the minimum threshold. You may want to contact **Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG)** at [ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg](mailto:ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg) to explore whether your startup might qualify under other eligible programmes they support. Question:
We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore and we want to know if we can qualify for the SEP bonus under COMPASS. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a VC that isn't EDBI or SEEDS Capital. Does that count, or does the funding have to come from specific investors?
Expected:
High-growth startups incorporated in Singapore can qualify for the SEP bonus if they have a minimum investment amount of USD 10 million over the past 36 months. The funding must have been received from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms (there is a separate list of recognised investment firms maintained for this purpose). So your startup would meet the investment threshold (USD 12 million exceeds the USD 10 million minimum) and the 36-month timeframe, but the VC firm you received funding from must be on the list of recognised investment firms. If it is not EDBI or SEEDS Capital, it needs to be one of the other firms on that recognised list. This programme is supported by Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG), and you can contact them at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg for queries, including whether your investor qualifies.
Actual:
Based on the COMPASS criteria, your startup would **not** qualify for the SEP bonus under the current eligible programmes. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/annex-c---eligible-programmes-for-sep-bonus.md]

The high-growth startup category requires two conditions to be met:
- Incorporation in Singapore ✓ (you meet this)
- Minimum investment of USD $10 million over the past 36 months ✓ (you meet this)
- **Funding from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms** ✗ (your VC funding does not qualify)

The funding source is a specific requirement—it must come from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms. A standard VC that isn't on this list does not satisfy the criterion, even if the investment amount exceeds the minimum threshold.

You may want to contact **Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG)** at [ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg](mailto:ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg) to explore whether your startup might qualify under other eligible programmes they support. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/index.md]

q-0013simple3We're hiring a 30-year-old candidate for a role in our banking company. What fixed monthly salary does he need to score points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion, and is there a way to get the maximum 20 points?For a 30-year-old candidate in the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities sector, here are the C1 salary benchmarks [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md]: - **10 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$11,251** or above (65th percentile) - **20 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$17,401** or above (90th percentile) Yes, there is a way to get the maximum 20 points—your candidate's fixed monthly salary must meet or exceed the 90th percentile benchmark of $17,401 per month.The chatbot correctly identified the exact salary thresholds ($11,251 for 10 points and $17,401 for 20 points) for a 30-year-old in the Banking sector, explained the points structure, and noted the EP qualifying salary requirement. While it initially asked for sector clarification (reasonable given sector-specific benchmarks), it provided the complete and accurate answer once the sector was confirmed.
Question:
We're hiring a 30-year-old candidate for a role in our banking company. What fixed monthly salary does he need to score points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion, and is there a way to get the maximum 20 points?
Expected:
For the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities n.e.c. sector, a 30-year-old candidate needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $11,251 to score 10 points under C1 (this is the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries in that sector). To score the maximum 20 points, the candidate needs at least $17,401 (the 90th percentile). These benchmarks were released in August 2025 and are updated once a year to reflect the latest market conditions. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $7,510 (10 points) / $11,151 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and rises to $19,266 (10 points) / $30,793 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. Additionally, candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points scored under C1. EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.
Actual:
For a 30-year-old candidate in the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities sector, here are the C1 salary benchmarks [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md]:

- **10 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$11,251** or above (65th percentile)
- **20 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$17,401** or above (90th percentile)

Yes, there is a way to get the maximum 20 points—your candidate's fixed monthly salary must meet or exceed the 90th percentile benchmark of $17,401 per month.

q-0014simple1I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.For an Employment Pass (EP) holder who does manual labour, here's what's actually required: **Medical Insurance:** Not mandatory. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/are-employers-required-to-buy-medical-insurance-for-employment-pass-holders.md] Providing medical insurance is optional—you can choose to include it in the employment contract or encourage your EP holder to obtain their own coverage. **Work Injury Compensation (WIC) Insurance:** Only mandatory if your EP holder earns less than $1,600 per month. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/does-an-employer-need-to-buy-wic-insurance-for-an-ep-holder.md] However, regardless of whether you've purchased insurance, you remain obligated to pay compensation for eligible workplace injury claims. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md] **Other obligations:** You must provide paid sick leave (including dental leave) under the Employment Act. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md] The key difference is that medical and WIC insurance requirements are stricter for Work Permit and S Pass holders, but EP holders have more flexibility.The chatbot incorrectly states WIC insurance is only mandatory if the EP holder earns less than $1,600/month, omitting that it is also mandatory for manual workers regardless of salary. Since the user specifically mentioned their EP holder does manual labour, this is a critical error that could mislead the employer into not purchasing required insurance.
Question:
I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.
Expected:
Providing medical insurance is not a requirement for employing Employment Pass (EP) holders. Most EP holders may already have their own medical insurance or may want to choose their own coverage. For EP holders without existing medical insurance, employers can choose to provide insurance coverage as part of the employment contract, or encourage them to get their own. The medical insurance requirement applies only to Work Permit and S Pass holders, in line with employers' existing responsibility for the upkeep and maintenance of their migrant workers, including the provision of medical treatment.

However, for work injury compensation (WIC) insurance, buying it is only mandatory if you are employing manual workers or non-manual workers earning less than $1,600 a month. So if your EP holder is a manual worker, you are required to purchase work injury compensation insurance for them. Whether or not you have purchased WIC insurance, you are still required to pay compensation for an eligible claim.

Additionally, under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA), you are obligated to pay any eligible claims for workplace injuries. You are also obligated to provide paid sick leave, including dental leave, to any employee covered under the Employment Act.
Actual:
For an Employment Pass (EP) holder who does manual labour, here's what's actually required:

**Medical Insurance:** Not mandatory. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/are-employers-required-to-buy-medical-insurance-for-employment-pass-holders.md] Providing medical insurance is optional—you can choose to include it in the employment contract or encourage your EP holder to obtain their own coverage.

**Work Injury Compensation (WIC) Insurance:** Only mandatory if your EP holder earns less than $1,600 per month. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/does-an-employer-need-to-buy-wic-insurance-for-an-ep-holder.md] However, regardless of whether you've purchased insurance, you remain obligated to pay compensation for eligible workplace injury claims. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md]

**Other obligations:** You must provide paid sick leave (including dental leave) under the Employment Act. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md]

The key difference is that medical and WIC insurance requirements are stricter for Work Permit and S Pass holders, but EP holders have more flexibility.

Question:
We have a candidate who's currently on a Dependant's Pass under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder — not the spouse, just a dependent. They want to work for us. Can they just get a Letter of Consent like spouses do, or do they need a separate work pass?
Expected:
Only spouses of Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holders can work or operate a business in Singapore with a Letter of Consent (LOC). Other dependants who are not spouses will need to obtain a work pass to work for an employer in Singapore. If the dependant obtains an EP or S Pass, they will be granted the work and stay privileges associated with that pass, their stay will no longer depend on the main Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, and they will need to cancel their Dependant's Pass before the EP or S Pass is issued. If the dependant obtains a Work Permit instead, they must hold on to both the Work Permit and the Dependant's Pass; the Work Permit validity will be tied to the DP's validity; they can apply for any Work Permit regardless of nationality or citizenship; they are not subject to the six-monthly medical examination, security bond, or pregnancy restrictions; and they do not need medical insurance from the employer if they are already covered under a medical insurance plan that meets MOM's prevailing minimum mandatory insurance coverage requirement.
Actual:
They need a separate work pass. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]

Letters of Consent are available only to spouses of Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holders. Other dependants must obtain a work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) to work for an employer in Singapore. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]

You'll need to have the candidate get a job offer and then apply for the appropriate work pass on their behalf based on their qualifications and your role requirements.

Correctly states LOC is only for spouses and that other dependants need a separate work pass (EP, S Pass, or Work Permit). However, it omits key details from the reference: the distinction between EP/S Pass (cancel DP) vs Work Permit (retain DP), WP validity tied to DP, nationality/citizenship exemptions, and exemptions from medical exams, security bonds, pregnancy restrictions, and medical insurance requirements.

Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus under the SOL. What are the requirements the candidate needs to meet for that specific role?
Expected:
For the Carbon credit trader role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both of the following criteria: (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields, and (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Additionally, Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that unlike many other SOL occupations where MOM verifies the firm's involvement in the relevant space directly with agencies, for this role it is EnterpriseSG that assesses the applications. The candidate also needs to perform the listed job duties, which include: trading or brokering carbon credits; originating and structuring carbon credit deals; facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits; and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors affecting price dynamics in OTC markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). You must also select the shortage occupation in the EP application, and the job title selected must match the occupation used for the job advertisement on MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.

q-0018simple1My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?To work in Singapore as a common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Here's what you need to do: **Application Process:** 1. Your prospective employer must download and complete the [Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form](https://www.mom.gov.sg/-/media/mom/documents/services-forms/passes/loc_cls_application_form.pdf) 2. Your employer submits the form with relevant supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md] **Timeline:** The application typically takes **within 4 weeks for most cases** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]. The LOC is valid up to the expiry date of your Long-Term Visit Pass, so ensure your pass has at least 3 months validity remaining before applying [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md].Correctly identifies LOC requirement and employer-driven application, but states processing time as 4 weeks instead of the reference's 8 weeks, which is a factual error on a key point. Also omits significant details about supporting documents, regulated occupations, and the up-to-60-month duration.
Question:
My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?
Expected:
As a common-law spouse holding a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) issued by MOM under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Your prospective employer must complete the application on your behalf by: (1) downloading and filling in the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form, and (2) submitting the completed form with the relevant supporting documents online at go.gov.sg/mom-submit-loc-cls. The form should be completed using Adobe Acrobat Reader DC, as it may not appear correctly with other PDF readers. The form can only be used if the LTVP was issued by the Ministry of Manpower. If the LTVP is valid for less than 3 months, it must be renewed before submitting the application. The duration applied for can be up to 60 months, though if approved, the period granted may be shorter than what was indicated. It takes around 8 weeks to process the application. Supporting documents required include: the original completed and signed application form (1 PDF, up to 7 MB), the travel document page showing the applicant's personal particulars (1 PDF or JPG, up to 1 MB), and — if applicable — an SFA licence for food establishments (1 PDF, up to 2 MB), a support letter from Enterprise Singapore for Representative Office applications (1 PDF, up to 3 MB), and registration or support letters from the relevant vetting agency/professional body/accreditation agency if the applicant will take on regulated occupations such as Dentist (Singapore Dental Council), Doctor (Singapore Medical Council), Emergency Medical Technician or Paramedic (Unit for Prehospital Emergency Care), Lawyer (Legal Services Regulatory Authority), Diagnostic radiographer/Occupational therapist/Physiotherapist/Radiation therapist/Speech therapist (Allied Health Professionals Council), Nurse (Singapore Nursing Board), Pharmacist (Singapore Pharmacy Council), TCM practitioner (Traditional Chinese Medicine Practitioners Board), or Football player/Coach (Sport Singapore) (1 PDF, up to 3 MB). Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation done by a translation service provider. MOM may also ask for additional documents during review. Note: Long-Term Visit Pass holders who are not common-law spouses cannot use this LOC process and must instead obtain a work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer, meeting all the criteria for the relevant pass.
Actual:

2. Your employer submits the form with relevant supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]

q-0020medium3We're bringing in an integrated circuit engineer for our semiconductor fab. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics. Since your candidate meets both criteria, you're on track. You don't need to submit proof of your semiconductor involvement; MOM will verify that directly with EDB. Question:
We're bringing in an integrated circuit engineer for our semiconductor fab. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics. I want to make sure we meet the requirements for this role under COMPASS — does the candidate need to satisfy both the semiconductor firm verification and the degree requirement, or is one sufficient? And do we need to submit any proof to MOM that we're in the semiconductor space?
Expected:
The candidate must meet both criteria simultaneously — it is not sufficient to satisfy only one. First, the candidate's employer must be a firm involved in the Semiconductor space; MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so no additional documentation from the employer is required. However, new entrants to the Semiconductor space can reach out to EDB. Second, the candidate must hold at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering or science faculty, which includes but is not limited to qualifications relating to those fields. A Bachelor's in Physics would fall under a science faculty and should satisfy the degree requirement. Since MOM verifies the firm's semiconductor involvement directly with EDB, the employer does not need to submit additional documentation for that criterion.

Question:
I'm applying for an Employment Pass for a cybersecurity engineer role. My candidate doesn't have a degree — he studied at a polytechnic — but he's been working as an ICT security architect for the past 4 years. Would he still qualify for the skills bonus under the shortage occupation list?

Question:
We have a candidate's spouse who already has a Letter of Consent to work for another company. Now the spouse also started their own business here. Can we apply for a separate LOC so the spouse can work for us as well?
Expected:
No. A spouse can only hold one LOC at a time, as they are only allowed to work for one employer. Furthermore, if the spouse owns a business, they are considered employed by their own company, which means they cannot apply for an additional LOC to work for another employer. So in this case, the spouse who owns a business is already treated as employed by that business and would not be eligible for a second LOC to work for your company.

q-0024medium1We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who's 30 years old. I want to understand the COMPASS salary benchmarks — what fixed monthly salary do we need to hit for the C1 criterion to score points, and what's the difference between the 10-point and 20-point thresholds at that age?# COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks To score points under the C1 criterion, your candidate's fixed monthly salary needs to meet or exceed the **65th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. The specific salary thresholds vary by sector and are updated annually. **Point thresholds:** - **20 points**: 90th percentile and above - **10 points**: 65th to less than 90th percentile - **0 points**: Below 65th percentile [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] To find the exact salary figures for your candidate's age and your organisation's sector, you'll need to download the **C1 salary benchmarks by sector** document (most recently released in August 2025) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. Note that the benchmarks increase progressively with age from age 23 to 45, so the thresholds for a 30-year-old will be specific to that age bracket. **Important:** Even if your candidate scores points under C1, they must still meet the separate EP qualifying salary requirement, or the application will likely be rejected [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].The chatbot correctly explains the percentile-based scoring structure and general framework but fails to provide the specific salary figures ($6,987 for 10 points and $10,620 for 20 points) and the difference ($3,633) that the user explicitly asked for and that the reference answer identifies as essential.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who's 30 years old. I want to understand the COMPASS salary benchmarks — what fixed monthly salary do we need to hit for the C1 criterion to score points, and what's the difference between the 10-point and 20-point thresholds at that age?
Expected:
For a candidate aged 30, the required salary for 10 COMPASS points (which corresponds to the 65th percentile of local PMETs) is $6,987, and the required salary for 20 points (the 90th percentile of local PMETs) is $10,620. The difference between the two thresholds at age 30 is $3,633.
Actual:
# COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks

To score points under the C1 criterion, your candidate's fixed monthly salary needs to meet or exceed the **65th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. The specific salary thresholds vary by sector and are updated annually.

**Point thresholds:**
- **20 points**: 90th percentile and above
- **10 points**: 65th to less than 90th percentile  
- **0 points**: Below 65th percentile

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

To find the exact salary figures for your candidate's age and your organisation's sector, you'll need to download the **C1 salary benchmarks by sector** document (most recently released in August 2025) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. Note that the benchmarks increase progressively with age from age 23 to 45, so the thresholds for a 30-year-old will be specific to that age bracket.

**Important:** Even if your candidate scores points under C1, they must still meet the separate EP qualifying salary requirement, or the application will likely be rejected [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].

Reimbursements — including any form of reimbursements for expenses incurred by the foreign employee in the course of employment — are not counted toward the qualifying fixed monthly salary for an Employment Pass. Separately, the employer is responsible for the upkeep and maintenance of the foreign employee in Singapore, including the provision of adequate food and medical treatment. Additionally, the EP application must be accompanied by an undertaking from the employer or a sponsor of the foreign employee in such terms as the Controller may determine, which formally commits the employer to these and other obligations.
Actual:
I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base.

The CONTEXT addresses employer obligations regarding medical costs and upkeep for foreign employees, but it does not contain information about:

- Whether reimbursements for work-related expenses can be counted toward qualifying salary for an Employment Pass
- Whether food and medical cost coverage can be counted as part of compensation for salary qualification purposes
- What formal commitments employers must make regarding support as part of the Employment Pass application

To get an accurate answer on these specific compensation and salary qualification questions, you would need to consult the Employment Pass eligibility and salary requirements documentation, or contact the Ministry of Manpower directly.

Question:
We're looking to hire a foreign specialist for a role that's on the Shortage Occupation List. Our candidate graduated from KU Leuven in Belgium. When we submit the EP application, do we just pick any job title, or is there a specific process we need to follow for the SOL role — and will the candidate's university help with the application?
Expected:
For a Shortage Occupation List (SOL) role, the SOL bonus recognises jobs requiring highly specialised skills that are in shortage in the local workforce, determined through a robust evaluation process considering industry needs and local workforce development efforts. To qualify for this bonus: (1) the candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation; (2) you must select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application (not just any title); and (3) the candidate must meet checks on additional job requirements if they fall into certain categories. Regarding the candidate's university, Katholieke Universiteit Leuven (KU Leuven) in Belgium is on the list of recognised institutions, which can support the EP application (e.g., for meeting qualification-related criteria). So the university credential from KU Leuven is recognised, but you still need to follow the specific SOL application requirements including selecting the correct eligible job title and ensuring the candidate meets the relevant job duty and additional requirement checks.
Actual:
For an SOL role, you cannot simply pick any job title—there is a specific process you must follow:

**Job Title Selection:**
You must select one of the **eligible job titles** listed for the specific shortage occupation in your EP application form. This job title must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture, unless you are exempted from the job advertising requirement. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]

**Additional Requirements:**
Your candidate must meet the additional requirements specific to that shortage occupation, which can include:
- Relevant qualifications (uploaded in the qualifications section)
- Work experience in relevant job titles (selected in the work experience section)
- Registration proof (uploaded in the agency support question) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]

The candidate must also perform the actual job duties listed for that specific shortage occupation. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

**Regarding the university:** The knowledge base does not contain information about whether the candidate's university (KU Leuven) plays a role in the application process. You should refer to the SOL Employer Guide or contact the relevant supporting sector agency for your specific occupation for clarification on qualification requirements.

Your candidate has four years as a Software Development Manager, which exceeds the three-year requirement, so the lack of a computer science degree is not a barrier. **Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth, family office or philanthropy):** No, this candidate does **not** qualify. Question:
We're looking to bring in a candidate on an EP for a Software Development Manager role. He doesn't have a degree in computer science or any related IT field — he studied mechanical engineering — but he does have about four years of experience as a Software Development Manager. Separately, we're also considering hiring a Relationship Manager for our family office private banking unit (we're a licensed FI with MAS). That candidate has two years of experience as a Relationship Manager in ultra-high net worth. Would both of these candidates qualify for the Skills Bonus under the shortage occupation list?
Expected:
For the Software Development Manager role, the candidate can qualify for the C5 Skills Bonus on the Shortage Occupation List even without a relevant degree, because the additional requirements allow either (1a) at least a Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies), OR (1b) a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of the eligible job titles (Application development manager, Head of applications development, Software and applications manager, or Software development manager). Since your candidate has four years of experience as a Software Development Manager, he satisfies criterion (1b) and would be eligible. The supporting agency is IMDA.

For the Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy) role, the candidate would NOT qualify. The additional requirements for that role require the candidate to meet BOTH criteria: (1) the employer must be a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (which your firm satisfies — MOM verifies this directly with MAS, no additional documentation needed), AND (2) the candidate must have a minimum total of three years' work experience as a Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy). Your candidate has only two years of experience, which falls short of the three-year minimum. The supporting agency is MAS.

So only the Software Development Manager candidate would qualify; the Relationship Manager candidate would not.

Your candidate has four years as a Software Development Manager, which exceeds the three-year requirement, so the lack of a computer science degree is not a barrier.

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/upcoming-changes-to-employment-pass-eligibility/complementarity-assessment-framework-compass/compass-shortage-occupation-list.md] The role requires candidates to meet **both** criteria: (1) your employer is a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (which you meet), and (2) **minimum total three years' work experience** in the role. The supporting agency for this programme is Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG), and you should contact them directly if you have any queries about your eligibility.

q-0032complex3We want to appoint one of our EP holders as a director on the board of a subsidiary company we own. After we get MOM's approval, what do we need to do next, and when the EP comes up for renewal, do we need to do anything about the directorship approval as well?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do: **After MOM approval:** Once you receive the Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, you need to [register the directorship position with ACRA](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). **Upon EP renewal:** Yes, you will need to take action. [When you have successfully renewed the EP, you must apply for a new LOC](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). The LOC's validity is limited to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so it does not automatically carry over to the renewed pass.The chatbot's answer covers all key points: registering with ACRA after receiving the LOC, the LOC's validity being tied to the current EP's expiry/cancellation, and the need to apply for a new LOC upon EP renewal. This is substantively equivalent to the reference answer.
Question:
We want to appoint one of our EP holders as a director on the board of a subsidiary company we own. After we get MOM's approval, what do we need to do next, and when the EP comes up for renewal, do we need to do anything about the directorship approval as well?
Expected:
After MOM grants the Letter of Consent (LOC), you must register the directorship position with ACRA. MOM will generally grant the LOC for directorship appointments in companies that are related by corporate shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer, as these appointments may be critical to their primary job role. Regarding renewal: the LOC is valid only up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so when you successfully renew the EP, you must apply for a new LOC for the directorship to continue.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do:

**After MOM approval:**
Once you receive the Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, you need to [register the directorship position with ACRA](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md).

**Upon EP renewal:**
Yes, you will need to take action. [When you have successfully renewed the EP, you must apply for a new LOC](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). The LOC's validity is limited to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so it does not automatically carry over to the renewed pass.
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer covers all key points: registering with ACRA after receiving the LOC, the LOC's validity being tied to the current EP's expiry/cancellation, and the need to apply for a new LOC upon EP renewal. This is substantively equivalent to the reference answer.

You must meet **one of these routes** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]: 1. **Salary-based route**: Earn a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months leading up to your application date, working for an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M). 2. **Outstanding achievements route**: If you don't meet the salary requirement, you may still qualify through outstanding achievements in academia and research (among other sectors). MOM and relevant agencies like MOE, NRF, and A*STAR will holistically review your work and key accomplishments [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]. ## Employer Financial Documentation The knowledge base does not specify which employer-related financial documents you need to prepare or their recency requirements. Question:
I'm a researcher considering applying for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. I know it covers multiple sectors, but I'm not sure how the eligibility assessment works — do I just need to meet some general criteria, or is it more specific than that? And what kind of employer-related financial documents would I need to prepare, and how recent do they have to be?
Expected:
The Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass covers top talent in all sectors, including academia and research, so you would be eligible to apply as a researcher. For eligibility, candidates are assessed on a case-by-case basis. You must choose the category that best fits you and meet all the criteria within that chosen category — not just some general criteria. Regarding employer-related financial documents, you need to provide the latest financial information of your employer, obtained within the last 3 months. This includes documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. Additionally, if you have income sources other than your fixed monthly salary, you must provide documents supporting those other income sources. You will also need employment verification proof from a background screening company.
Actual:
# Eligibility Assessment and Documentation for Researchers

## How Eligibility Assessment Works

The assessment is **specific to your situation**, not just general criteria. You must meet **one of these routes** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]:

1. **Salary-based route**: Earn a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months leading up to your application date, working for an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M).

2. **Outstanding achievements route**: If you don't meet the salary requirement, you may still qualify through outstanding achievements in academia and research (among other sectors). MOM and relevant agencies like MOE, NRF, and A*STAR will holistically review your work and key accomplishments [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md].

They can work in Singapore by applying for a Letter of Consent (LOC) — the employer completes the application on their behalf [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. **22-year-old unmarried son with disability:** An LTVP [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. The 22-year-old unmarried handicapped son would also be eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass, as handicapped children who are unmarried and aged 21 and above qualify for an LTVP. The 19-year-old unmarried stepchild would likewise qualify for a Long-Term Visit Pass, since stepchildren who are unmarried and under 21 years old are eligible for an LTVP. Note that the LTVP for the handicapped son and the stepchild does not mention eligibility for a Letter of Consent to work — only spouses and common-law spouses on their respective passes are described as being able to apply for an LOC to work in Singapore.

Question:
We're filing an LTVP application for a ONE Pass holder's spouse who wants to work in Singapore. The spouse has support from the Singapore Medical Council, and most of their documents — including the SMC support letter — are in Mandarin. Also, the spouse previously entered Singapore on a passport from a different country than the one they're using now. How should we handle all of this on the application?
Expected:
There are several things to address in this application: (1) Since the applicant has obtained support from the Singapore Medical Council, which is one of the listed Vetting Agencies on the form, you must select 'Yes' to indicate vetting agency support and select 'Singapore Medical Council' from the list. You must also produce documentary proof of support from the Singapore Medical Council together with the application. (2) All non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation, and the translation can be done by a translation service provider. So the Mandarin documents, including the SMC support letter if it is in Mandarin, need to have English translations attached. (3) In Part 5 – Other Information of the application form, the applicant must declare 'Yes' to the question asking whether they have entered Singapore using a passport issued by a different country, since that applies to their situation. Additionally, by submitting the application, the applicant consents to the Government of Singapore and statutory authorities obtaining from and verifying information with any person, organisation, or any other source.

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]

**Document Translation:** Non-English documents must be accompanied by English translations, and "the translation can be done by a translation service provider." This applies to the Mandarin SMC support letter and any other non-English documents you're submitting. q-0037complex1My company is terminating an EP holder and their last day is 15 February. Also, if MOM asks us for extra documents during the process, how do we send those in? And one more thing — after cancellation, if the person still has their physical EP card, what should we do with it?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to know: **Timing of cancellation submission:** You can submit your EP cancellation request up to 14 days in advance. Since the last day is 15 February, you can submit from 1 February onwards. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md] **Extending stay after cancellation:** You can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) for the pass holder upon cancellation, which will grant them valid stay in Singapore for up to 90 days. This allows them to remain in Singapore to settle personal matters while their EP is cancelled. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md] **Submitting additional documents:** I don't have information in the knowledge base about the process for submitting extra documents if MOM requests them during the cancellation process. **Physical EP card:** After cancellation, you should cut the card in half and discard it to prevent misuse. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md] **Important reminder:** Before cancelling, ensure you've given reasonable notice, sought tax clearance from IRAS at least 1 month before the last day of employment, settled all outstanding employment issues including salary, and arranged repatriation (air ticket and transport costs).
