q-0066hard-21My candidate graduated from a Singapore institution and we want to verify their qualification through an online portal instead of using a background screening company. I've heard there's a platform called OpenCerts that can be used. How exactly do we upload the certificate file on that platform, and what do we need to capture in the screenshot for the EP application?Based on the knowledge base, I can confirm that OpenCerts is indeed a recognized verification method for Employment Pass applications. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image157.png] However, I don't have enough information to answer the specific technical details about how to upload certificate files on the OpenCerts platform itself or what exactly needs to be captured in screenshots for the EP application. The CONTEXT shows that OpenCerts is one of three verification method options available (alongside background screening companies and government/awarding institution verification portals) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image4.png], and that digital certificates uploaded through OpenCerts must not exceed 2MB in file size [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image4.png]. For detailed step-by-step instructions on using the OpenCerts platform itself, you may need to consult OpenCerts' own documentation or contact MOM directly for guidance.The chatbot acknowledges OpenCerts as a valid verification method and mentions the 2MB file size limit, but fails to provide the key details: drag-and-drop or 'Select File' upload methods, and the requirement for two screenshots (verification page and verified page) with visible website addresses and candidate information.
Question:
My candidate graduated from a Singapore institution and we want to verify their qualification through an online portal instead of using a background screening company. I've heard there's a platform called OpenCerts that can be used. How exactly do we upload the certificate file on that platform, and what do we need to capture in the screenshot for the EP application?
Expected:
On the OpenCerts platform, you can verify your candidate's certificate by dragging and dropping the opencert file into the upload area to view its contents, or by clicking the 'Select File' button to browse for the file. For the Employment Pass application, when submitting verification proof from an awarding institution's verification portal, you need to upload two screenshots: (1) a verification page screenshot that includes the visible website address of the verification portal and all information entered such as the candidate's name and date of birth, and (2) a verified page screenshot that includes the visible website address and the final page showing that the candidate was awarded the educational qualification. Both the verification page and verified page screenshots should be uploaded to the EP application form.

q-0068hard-23We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?Based on the Fair Consideration Framework requirements, here's the timeline for your EP application: **Earliest submission date:** You can submit the EP application 15 days after posting the job ad. Since you posted in early January, you could apply around mid-January. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md] **Deadline for using that ad:** You must submit the EP application within 3 months of the job ad closing. Since your ad closed in early February, the deadline would be around early May. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md] Note that the ad itself must remain open for at least 14 consecutive days to allow candidates to apply, which your 30-day posting period satisfies. Question:
We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?
Expected:
You can submit the EP or S Pass application at the earliest 15 days after posting the job ad. Once the job ad closes (in this example, after 30 days around early February), you have 3 months from the closing date to submit the EP or S Pass application using that job ad. After that 3-month window (in this example, around early May), the advertisement can no longer be used and you would need to post a new one. Additionally, the job ad must be open for at least 14 consecutive days before you can submit the application.

q-0069hard-21We're putting together an EP application for a candidate age 30 who'll be joining our fund management firm. I want to know the minimum fixed monthly salary needed to score 10 points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion. Also, what salary would get us the full 20 points? And just to confirm — which government body publishes these benchmarks?To answer your question, I need to clarify that the specific salary thresholds for C1 points depend on your organisation's sector classification and the candidate's age. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md] **How C1 points are awarded:** - **20 points**: Salary at or above the 90th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector - **10 points**: Salary at the 65th to below 90th percentile - **0 points**: Below the 65th percentile [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] **To find your exact salary thresholds:** You'll need to consult the C1 salary benchmarks by sector, which are updated annually. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md] The benchmarks are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department's annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey and are released each August. You can download the current benchmarks for your fund management sector to see the exact 65th and 90th percentile figures for a 30-year-old candidate. **Publisher:** The Ministry of Manpower (MOM) publishes these benchmarks. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]The chatbot correctly identifies MOM as the publisher and explains the percentile framework, but fails to provide the specific salary figures ($13,030 for 10 points and $21,401 for 20 points) that the user explicitly asked for, instead directing them to look up the benchmarks themselves.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate age 30 who'll be joining our fund management firm. I want to know the minimum fixed monthly salary needed to score 10 points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion. Also, what salary would get us the full 20 points? And just to confirm — which government body publishes these benchmarks?
Expected:
For the Fund Management Activities & Activities Auxiliary to Financial Services sector, a candidate aged 30 needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $13,030 to score 10 points under C1 (meeting the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries), and at least $21,401 to score 20 points (meeting the 90th percentile of local PMET salaries). These benchmarks are from the release dated August 2025 and are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department's (MRSD) annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 up to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $8,917 (10 points) / $12,474 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and goes up to $21,845 (10 points) / $40,530 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. The benchmarks are published by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM). Note that candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points, and EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.

**How C1 points are awarded:**
- **20 points**: Salary at or above the 90th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector
- **10 points**: Salary at the 65th to below 90th percentile
- **0 points**: Below the 65th percentile

q-0071hard-22We're preparing a Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder. The applicant will be partially paid by both a local and an overseas employer. When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?Based on the knowledge base, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary and handles the scenario you're describing: **Definition of Fixed Monthly Salary:** Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md] **When Fixed Monthly Salary Exceeds Basic Monthly Salary:** If the amount you declare as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will treat the difference as 'fixed monthly allowances'. For example, if you declare S$5,000 as fixed monthly salary and S$4,500 as basic monthly salary, MOM will recognize S$500 as fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md] **Important Note:** The form emphasizes that fixed monthly salary includes only basic monthly salary and fixed monthly allowances—not other forms of compensation. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the fixed monthly salary amount should exactly match the basic monthly salary [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]Covers the definition of fixed monthly salary, the treatment of the difference as fixed monthly allowances, and the case where there are no allowances accurately. However, it does not address the 'salary payable by' options (Local, Overseas, Both local and overseas) which the reference answer includes as relevant to the user's scenario of being partially paid by both a local and overseas employer.
Question:
We're preparing a Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder. The applicant will be partially paid by both a local and an overseas employer. When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?
Expected:
According to the LOC (Common-Law Spouse) Application Form, fixed monthly salary is defined as basic monthly salary plus fixed monthly allowances. For example, S$5,000 fixed monthly salary = S$4,500 basic monthly salary + S$500 fixed monthly allowances. MOM will use the fixed monthly salary to assess the application. If the amount indicated as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will take the difference as the 'fixed monthly allowances'. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the amount of fixed monthly salary should be exactly the same as the basic monthly salary. For the salary payable by, there are three options: 'Local' (paid by a Singapore-registered employer or paid by an overseas employer which is then charged fully to a Singapore-registered employer), 'Overseas' (employer is not registered in Singapore or is a representative office registered with Enterprise Singapore), or 'Both local and overseas' (partially paid by both local and overseas employer).
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary and handles the scenario you're describing:

**Definition of Fixed Monthly Salary:**
Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]

**When Fixed Monthly Salary Exceeds Basic Monthly Salary:**
If the amount you declare as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will treat the difference as 'fixed monthly allowances'. For example, if you declare S$5,000 as fixed monthly salary and S$4,500 as basic monthly salary, MOM will recognize S$500 as fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]

**Important Note:**
The form emphasizes that fixed monthly salary includes only basic monthly salary and fixed monthly allowances—not other forms of compensation. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the fixed monthly salary amount should exactly match the basic monthly salary [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]
